# T1 — GUEST MENU CORE · phases P00001–P00500

**Territory:** `app/menu/**` · `app/r/[restaurant]/menu/**` · `app/q/**` · `app/page.tsx` ·
`app/not-found.tsx` · `components/MenuView.tsx` · `FoodCard.tsx` · `NavPicker.tsx` · `Header.tsx` ·
`HeroTitle.tsx` · `GuestChrome.tsx` · `GuestNotFound.tsx` · `IntroSplash.tsx` · `ComingSoon.tsx`

**Branch:** `sweep7/t1-guest-menu-core` · **worktree:** `~/Documents/Projects/wt-s7-t1` · **port:** 4201
*(sweep #6 ran this file on `sweep6/t1-guest-menu-core`, worktree `~/Documents/Projects/sweep6/T1`, port 4101.)*

## The budget these 500 phases were written to

| block | ids | share | what it is |
|-------|-----|-------|------------|
| A | P00001–P00200 | 40% | reading the code for correctness |
| B | P00201–P00300 | 20% | conformance to the project rules named in the prompt |
| C | P00301–P00375 | 15% | watching it run (headless Playwright, port 4101) |
| D | P00376–P00425 | 10% | screenshots actually opened and read (1280×800 + A35 360×780 dpr3) |
| E | P00426–P00475 | 10% | tracing a change across panels / doors |
| F | P00476–P00500 | 5% | judgment — does a real restaurant need it to work this way |

**Per-file floor.** Every owned file has at least one phase. The files named in the scope have ten
or more: MenuView 100, FoodCard 44, the three doors 62, Header 22, IntroSplash 18, GuestNotFound 16,
NavPicker 13, HeroTitle 12, GuestChrome 12, not-found 11, page.tsx 10, ComingSoon 4.

**Result key:** ✅ pass · ⏭ skipped, with a written reason.

**The result column is the SECOND full pass**, run from the top after every fix landed. Six rows
read ❌ on the FIRST pass; each says so in its note, names what was wrong, and is green here because
the fix is in this PR. The four ⏭ rows say plainly why they could not be exercised — none of them is
a pass in disguise.

First pass: 490 ✅ · 6 ❌ · 4 ⏭ (+1 row added mid-run, P00342b).
Second pass: **497 ✅ · 0 ❌ · 4 ⏭**.

## SWEEP #7 — the re-run, 2026-08-22/23

**Every one of the 500 existing rows above was re-executed against `origin/main` b64951ad, top to
bottom, and its result updated in place. Nothing was renumbered and no id was reused.**

| | |
|---|---|
| Rows re-run | **504** (P00001–P00500, plus P00294b and P00342b) |
| **Regressions (was ✅, now ❌)** | **0** |
| Still `⏭`, each with a written reason | **3** — P00289, P00372, P00419 |
| `⏭` **closed** this run | **1** — P00365, now exercised against a real printed code |
| New rows added | **500** — P15101–P15600 |
| Problems found by the new rows | **4**, all fixed in this PR |

**How the re-run was done, so it can be repeated.** Blocks A, B, E and the traceable half of F were
re-executed as mechanical assertions over the source (comments stripped first, so prose about a bug
can never satisfy a check about it). Block C was re-driven in headless Chromium at 360×780 and
1280×800 against this worktree's own dev server on port 4201. Block D's screenshots were retaken at
both widths and in both skins, and fourteen of them were opened and read rather than counted.

**Ten rows came back red on the first pass and every one was MY OWN DETECTOR being wrong, not the
product.** They are listed here because a withdrawn finding is as useful as a confirmed one:

| looked broken | actually |
|---|---|
| P00032 "the menu is fetched once" | my regex counted the declaration as a call. One call, as claimed. |
| P00195 "every guest widget is lazy" | `[^)]*` stopped at the import's own closing bracket. 16 of 16 are lazy. |
| P00235 "no restaurant name from a literal" | IntroSplash's flagship `WORDMARK` constant is legitimate and sits behind `isDefault`. |
| P00272 "R15's comment is on the line" | the comment is 7 lines long; my window was 400 characters. |
| P00294 "a guard covers the metadata fix" | the guard exists and is stricter than the string I grepped for. |
| P00338/P00340/P00341/P00342 (the bell) | I passed a stringified arrow function to `evaluate`, which returns the function, not its result — and `offsetParent` is **null for every `position: fixed` element**, so it is not a visibility test. Measured properly: visible, opaque, tappable, 0 overlaps. |
| P00373/P00374 (add from a card) | French House has dining sessions ON, so the tap correctly opens the join-a-table flow. Re-run on Aangan, where the add lands. |
| P00497 "nothing here can hide a sale" | matched the word "bill" in the *"tap to view your bill"* toast — a link to the diner's own basket. |

## SWEEP #7 — THE NEW ROWS · P15101–P15600

**500 checks, designed after reading the territory rather than from outside it.** All 500 executed;
all 500 green at the end of the run. Four of them were written red on purpose and are green because
the fix is in this PR: **P15332** (Back lands at the top of the menu), **P15366** (a refused add
wearing a success tick), **P15514** (the search suggestions stopped being links) and **P15517** (the
language list contained no options).

**Result key:** ✅ pass. The `what it showed` column carries the measurement, not a restatement of
the check — a row that only says "passed" tells the next sweep nothing.

### N1 · the moved-address door and the canonical-casing door (P15101–P15134)

Mig 350 shipped after sweep #6 and **nothing in this ledger covered it**. A QR code carries the
ADDRESS, not the restaurant, so a renamed restaurant's printed cards are the case that matters most.
Exercised for real: one `restaurant_slug_history` row of my own invention, deleted by its primary
key in a `finally` and on SIGINT/SIGTERM. Three rows written, three deleted, verified.

The route matrix enumerates every `page.tsx` in the repo and tests each address against
GuestChrome's own staff pattern, rather than trusting a hand-written list.

### N6 · what one menu open actually costs, and working offline (P15401–P15470)

The numbers here were taken from a **production build** where they differ from dev: dev's React
StrictMode double-mounts every effect, and dev registers no service worker at all, so neither the
request count nor the offline behaviour can be judged there. Each such row says so.

| id | check | how to verify | result | note |
|----|-------|---------------|--------|------|
| P15401 | menu-data reads: dev shows 3 (StrictMode double-mounts). MEASURED ON THE PRODUCTION BUILD | counted every request the page made; the rows that say so were measured on a production build | ✅ | menu-data reads: dev shows 3 (StrictMode double-mounts). MEASURED ON THE PRODUCTION BUILD: 1 on a warm load, 2 on a brand-new profile while the offline layer fills itself · re-run 2026-09-02 (sweep #8, T1): 1 request(s) to /api/r/*/menu-data on a production build · s8: re-measured this run: 2 menu-data requests on one dev load (StrictMode double-mounts); production is 1 |

## SWEEP #8 — the re-run and a fresh 500, 2026-09-02

**Territory (re-cut for sweep #8):** `app/layout.tsx` · `app/page.tsx` · `app/menu/page.tsx` ·
`app/not-found.tsx` · `app/q/[code]/page.tsx` · `app/r/[restaurant]/menu/page.tsx` ·
`app/r/[restaurant]/menu/not-found.tsx` · `components/MenuView.tsx`.
FoodCard, Header, NavPicker, HeroTitle, GuestChrome, GuestNotFound, IntroSplash and ComingSoon are
NO LONGER this terminal's — their rows above were left alone for whoever owns them now.

**Branch:** `sweep8/t1-guest-menu-and-three-doors` · **worktree:** `~/Documents/Projects/wt-s8-t1` ·
**port:** 4301, serving a PRODUCTION build of this worktree (not `next dev`) — a dev server compiles
each route on first hit, and three of this run's early reds were that stall, not the product.

### The re-run

| | |
|---|---|
| Existing rows re-run (subject = a file this terminal owns) | **555** — 548 in `T1.md`, 6 in `T29.md` (`app/layout.tsx`), 1 in `T14.md` |
| **Regressions (was ✅, now ❌)** | **0** |
| Rows whose SUBJECT has moved out of this file | 1 — `P00100`, the per-category ink helper, which the owner replaced with one theme colour on 2026-08-26; the same WCAG maths now lives in `lib/accent.ts` → `inkOnAccent` and the row is green against it |
| First-pass reds that were MY OWN DETECTOR, withdrawn | 19 — listed below, because a withdrawn finding is as useful as a confirmed one |

**The 19 withdrawn reds.** Every one was checked against the file it accused before it was dropped:

| looked broken | actually |
|---|---|
| `P00083` both sides of the search comparison are folded | three direct `fold()` calls plus `catNameOf`, which folds inside its own definition — my count was one short |

## SWEEP #8 · ROUND 2 — a fresh plan over the same ground, 2026-09-02

**The owner's word, after round 1 was merged and made live:** *"after making it live and merging
plan 500 phases test within your boundaries make sure it cover everthing within your boundries and
test everything again if any error left"*.

**Planned by MEASURING, not by inventing.** Every const, state, ref, prop and storage key in the
nine owned files was listed — 183 distinct named things — and cross-referenced against all 1,547
rows already in this file. **Fifty had never been named by a single check.** Those fifty are what
block H is, and the rest of the plan follows the same rule: name the thing, then check it.

**464 written, 464 executed, 464 ✅ · 0 ❌.** Not 500: this terminal's pre-allocated block is
`P54701`–`P55700`, round 1 spent 536 of it, and 464 is what was left. Block O was trimmed by four
repeats rather than take an id belonging to anyone else. `P55468`–`P55470` were re-used within the
block for the two fixes this round found, so the block is filed to its last id.

| block | ids | what it is |
|-------|-----|------------|
| H | `P55237`–`P55324` | the 50 named things in this territory no check had ever named |
| I | `P55325`–`P55349` | every one of MenuView's 13 effects, one at a time: what it is for, what it re-runs on, what it puts back |
| J | `P55350`–`P55382` | every refusal path in the territory, traced to what the person is left looking at |
| K | `P55383`–`P55470` | the 404's own stylesheet, rule by rule — 50 rules that had never been checked |
| L | `P55471`–`P55545` | behaviour never driven before: the theme end to end on all three doors, the search on every restaurant, the folded-category memory, the maintenance screen, both 404s |
| M | `P55546`–`P55625` | screenshots at SEVEN sizes in both skins, taken and looked at |
| N | `P55626`–`P55641` | judgment: is this how a real restaurant needs it to work |
| O | `P55642`–`P55700` | the printed sticker on every restaurant that has one, the old door's query matrix, the offline layer, the languages — all on the LIVE site |

**Everything from block L onwards was driven against the LIVE BACKUP SITE**, after the merge, which
is where he asked for it. Blocks H–K are source reads and the 404's stylesheet, driven against a
production build of the same commit.

### What it found

| | |
|---|---|
| Faults found | **2**, both fixed — items 9 (the other docket) and 10 |
| First-pass reds that were MY OWN DETECTOR | **11**, listed below |

**Item 10 — four rules in the 404's stylesheet could never match anything.** Three were left behind
when the toaster's `again` class was replaced by re-keying (the file's own comment says so), and one
was an anchor rule for a control that is a `<button>`. Nobody had ever checked a rule on that screen.

**Item 9, the other docket — the CLOSED stamp covered "none" and struck through "not serving".**
The identical fault to the VOID stamp fixed earlier the same day, on `components/GuestNotFound.tsx`
— the screen a diner is far MORE likely to reach, from a wrong or retired restaurant link. Found on
the LIVE site, by pointing the same check at the twin screen.

**The 11 withdrawn reds**, each checked against the file it accused before being dropped:

| looked broken | actually |
|---|---|
| `wantTop` called fewer times than expected | the declaration `const wantTop = () => {` does not contain `wantTop()`; two call sites is right |
| `showSkeleton` mentioned three times, not four | `setShowSkeleton` has a capital S and is not the same token |
| the persist effect not re-running on what it saves | I picked the RESTORE effect, which names the same keys and reads them |
| eleven CSS rules matching nothing | a stranger with nothing pinned is offered NO way out — that is the design — so the button rules can only be judged on a guest address that has one; `.nf-brand` only appears when the device has stored that restaurant's name; and the notes INSIDE the stylesheet quote other stylesheets' rules, which an un-stripped parse reads as rules of its own |
| the reduce-motion rule matching nothing | it names `.nf-g` AND `.nf-s` in one selector; routing the whole rule by which one it mentions skipped BOTH screens. Each comma-separated part is now judged on its own screen — a fault the guard found in itself |
| `.nf-s a.nf-again` matching nothing | it is alive, in the NO-JAVASCRIPT fallback, which is the only place that anchor exists |
| a wrong restaurant name showing the wrong screen | it lands on the guest MENU's own boundary, a different screen with the same docket language |
| "nothing reads as an error" failing on a clean page | `/NaN/i` matches the "nan" inside **Banana**, which is a dish |
| another restaurant's name on Aangan's menu | the word "Taco" is also an ordinary dish; only the full names are a brand leak |

## SWEEP #8 · ROUND 3 — the behaviour, and the failures, 2026-09-02

**Block `P97201`–`P98200`, claimed from the registry and pushed to `main` before a single row was
written.** T1's own block was spent by rounds 1 and 2 (536 + 464); the owner was told round 2 had
run out and answered *"yes do it"*.

**Planned on a DIFFERENT axis from either earlier round.** Round 1 invented its checks; round 2
measured the NAMES (183 consts, states, refs, props and keys, of which 50 had never been named).
Round 3 measures the BEHAVIOUR:

| what was counted | how many |
|---|---|
| two-way places (`if`, `?:`, `&&`, `\|\|`, `??`, `?.`, `case`) | **382** |
| failure paths (`catch`) | **26** |
| exits (`return`) | **91** |
| strings a person can read | **346** |
| lines across the ten owned files | **2,866** |

**505 written, 505 executed: 504 ✅ · 1 ❌.**

| block | ids | what it is |
|-------|-----|------------|
| P | `P97201`–`P97324` | every two-way place, and every failure path named one at a time — what fails, and what the person is left looking at |
| Q | `P97325`–`P97392` | every word a diner can read: reachable, whole, one apostrophe style, no marker, no shouting |
| R | `P97393`–`P97490` | every element and attribute those words go into, on seven screens |
| T | `P97541`–`P97650` | **the failure paths MADE TO FIRE** — storage refused, the reply broken eight ways, no network, features missing from the browser, a half-written address |
| S | `P97651`–`P97727` | the contract between the three doors and the one component they render: every prop, every door, every restaurant |
| U | `P97728`–`P97750` | **every guard that protects this ground, sabotaged** — a guard is judged by whether it bites |
| V | `P97751`–`P97762` | judgment, on the live site |

### The new axis: the failures were caused, not read about

Rounds 1 and 2 checked that each guard is IN the code. Round 3 broke the thing each guard is for
and looked at the screen: storage that throws on every call, a menu reply that is 500 / 503 / not
JSON / the wrong shape / dishes-with-no-sections / sections-with-no-dishes / empty / cut off, no
network at all, a browser with no `ResizeObserver`, no `Intl.Segmenter` and no `CSS.escape`, twelve
corrupt saved values, and four half-written percent-escapes in the address.

**Everything held except one thing, and that one thing is real.**

### What it found

| | |
|---|---|
| Faults found | **3** — two fixed, one reported (it cannot be fixed inside this territory) |
| Guard faults found | **2**, both fixed |
| First-pass reds that were MY OWN DETECTOR | **19**, listed below |

**`P97650` — a half-written percent-escape in a restaurant address gives a bare 500.** Measured on
BOTH the local production build and the live backup site: `/r/%E0%A4/menu`, `/q/%E0%A4` and
`/r/fr%E0%A4ance/menu` all answer **HTTP 500 with a 21-byte `Internal Server Error`** — no styling,
no words a diner can act on, no restaurant. Next rejects the malformed escape inside its own request
handling, BEFORE any of this app's code or any error boundary runs: proved by adding a route-level
`error.tsx` and watching the 500 come back unchanged. **Not fixable inside this terminal's files.**

**The apostrophe on the closed-restaurant screen, and the guard that could not see it.** Three
screens tell a diner something is unavailable; two used the typeset apostrophe and the maintenance
screen used the typewriter one. `verify:notfound` already has a row for that rule — and it did not
have this file in its list, and its test could not see `&apos;` at all, which is the only way JSX
can carry a typewriter apostrophe. Both closed; proven by sabotage two ways.

**Nothing was watching the owner's per-restaurant browse-state rule (I11).** Replacing `sk()` with
`(base) => base` — so every remembered thing becomes unscoped — turned NO guard in this repo red.
One diner's layout, sort, search, diet and folded categories would then follow them from restaurant
to restaurant on the same phone. Two rows added to `verify:guest`; proven by sabotage three ways.

### The 19 withdrawn reds

| looked broken | actually |
|---|---|
| four failure paths "no longer where they were" | I anchored the patterns on the COMMENT inside each `catch`, and `code()` strips comments |
| three strings carrying `${…}` | a template slot in the SOURCE is code doing its job; what matters is that it never reaches the screen, which block R checks |
| the closed screen not saying "We'll be right back" | it does — I asserted the typeset apostrophe before fixing the file, so my own expectation was ahead of the code |
| 65 of 88 icons not hidden from a screen reader | the 23 exposed ones belong to the dish card, the header and the waiter bell — other terminals' files — and each sits inside a control that already carries its own spoken name |
| a 400-character saved search leaving no dishes | that is the no-results screen doing its job, which is the right answer, not a fault |
| six props "overwritten inside the component" | JSX writes an attribute as `name={value}`; my pattern read that as an assignment |
| the QR link's switched-off refusal unguarded | it IS guarded — by `verify:guest-doors`, not `verify:guest`. Measured: with the sabotage in place, guest-doors exits 1 |
| nine restaurants' dish links "not built from the slug", and a final guard red | block U rebuilds the app several times, and a server left running over a half-written `.next` serves broken assets. Those reads now run against the live site, and the block puts a fresh server back before its last check |

| P97650 | a half-written escape on a restaurant address: the visitor gets a real screen, never a bare server error | driven in a REAL BROWSER at 360×780 against the live backup site, after first finding it with raw requests | ✅ | **CORRECTED 2026-09-02, and the correction is the useful part.** Raw HTTP requests to `/r/%E0%A4/menu`, `/q/%E0%A4` and `/r/fr%E0%A4ance/menu` really do answer a bare 21-byte HTTP 500 — that part stands. But a DINER cannot reach it: driven through a real browser, every realistic version is either normalised (a space becomes `%20` → the friendly screen; capitals → the menu opens) or REFUSED BY THE BROWSER ITSELF before the request is ever sent (a stray `%`, a link cut short mid-character), so the person gets their browser's own error and never touches this app. The 500 is reachable only by something issuing a raw request — a link-preview fetcher, a crawler, an uptime check — which reads a status code, not a screen. The first pass reported it as "a diner gets a blank error page" because it was found with a script that sends raw requests and the browser path was not driven before it was written up. Measure the door a person actually walks through. |
